	\subsection{Community Venue Membership (C1--C3)}
	\label{sec:problem12-community-venue-membership-c1-c3}

	A community organisation must select standard or expanded venue membership before booking demand is known to be high or low. Reservations are forecast at \(6\) bookings for standard membership under high demand and \(3\) under low demand, while expanded membership enables \(8\) and \(4\) bookings, respectively.

	The market supports \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} of \(10\) thousand USD per booking in the high-demand state and \(7\) in the low-demand state. A sponsor also contributes a fixed \(15\) thousand USD when the standard plan is selected or \(22\) for the expanded plan. Annual membership itself \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs} a fixed \(28\) thousand USD for the standard plan and \(40\) for the expanded plan, while permits add a state-based fixed \GTenToneHL{cost}{cost} of \(8\) under high demand or \(13\) under low demand. The organisation must calculate \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} and subtract both fixed \GTenToneHL{cost}{cost} sources without multiplying them by bookings.

	\textbf{Questions/Tasks.}
	\begin{enumerate}
		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem12-community-venue-membership-c1}{1}
		{Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}

		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem12-community-venue-membership-c3}{3}
		{Calculating profit and building the payoff table.}
	\end{enumerate}

	\subsubsection*{C1 - Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}
	\phantomsection
	\label{subsec:problem12-community-venue-membership-c1}

	\DecisionElementsTable
	{Select the standard venue membership; Select the expanded venue membership}
	{High booking demand; Low booking demand}
	{Booking demand revealed after the membership-plan decision}
	{Profit in thousand USD for each membership--demand pair}

	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{Revenue} contains a state-based variable component and an \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based
	fixed component:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{R}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i}).
	\]

	\GTenToneHL{cost}{Cost} contains \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based fixed components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{C}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	Therefore,
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{payoff}{P}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
	\right]
	-
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right].
	\]

	\secInicio[sec:problem12-community-venue-membership-c1-c3][sec:two-component-models]

	\subsection{Urban Delivery Fleet (C1--C3)}
	\label{sec:problem13-urban-delivery-fleet-c1-c3}

	An urban-delivery company must deploy conventional vans or electric cargo bikes before conditions resolve into high demand with light traffic or low demand with heavy traffic. Route forecasts assign the vans \(9\) delivery batches in the first state and \(5\) in the second, while cargo bikes can complete \(12\) and \(6\) batches, respectively.

	Customer contracts pay \(8\) thousand USD per batch in the high-demand state and \(6\) in the low-demand state. Meeting state targets also brings a fixed performance payment of \(14\) thousand USD with high demand or \(8\) with low demand. Van operation \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs} \(3\) thousand USD per batch and cargo-bike operation \(2\); traffic then adds \(1\) thousand USD per batch in light traffic or \(3\) in heavy traffic. The company must combine the fleet-based and traffic-based rates for every delivered batch before deducting \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs}.

	\textbf{Questions/Tasks.}
	\begin{enumerate}
		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem13-urban-delivery-fleet-c1}{1}
		{Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}

		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem13-urban-delivery-fleet-c3}{3}
		{Calculating profit and building the payoff table.}
	\end{enumerate}

	\subsubsection*{C1 - Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}
	\phantomsection
	\label{subsec:problem13-urban-delivery-fleet-c1}

	\DecisionElementsTable
	{Use a conventional van fleet; Use an electric cargo-bike fleet}
	{High demand with light traffic; Low demand with heavy traffic}
	{Delivery demand and traffic conditions revealed after the fleet decision}
	{Profit in thousand USD for each fleet--market-state pair}

	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{Revenue} contains state-based variable and fixed components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{R}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	\GTenToneHL{cost}{Cost} contains \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based variable components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{C}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right].
	\]

	Therefore,
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{payoff}{P}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right]
	-
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right].
	\]

	\secInicio[sec:problem13-urban-delivery-fleet-c1-c3][sec:two-component-models]

	\subsection{Data-Service Contract (C1--C3)}
	\label{sec:problem14-data-service-contract-c1-c3}

	A data-services company must choose standard or automated processing before a contract is confirmed as standard-volume or high-volume. Work estimates assign standard processing \(4\) batches under standard volume and \(7\) under high volume, while automation handles \(5\) and \(9\) batches, respectively.

	The method agreement pays fixed \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} of \(55\) thousand USD for standard processing or \(72\) for automation. The client adds a service-level payment of \(16\) thousand USD in the standard-volume state and \(9\) in the high-volume state. Processing \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs} \(6\) thousand USD per batch with the standard method or \(4\) with automation, and implementation adds a method-based fixed \GTenToneHL{cost}{cost} of \(18\) thousand USD or \(31\), respectively. The company must keep both \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} payments fixed while applying only the processing rate to batch \GTenToneHL{quantity}{quantity}.

	\textbf{Questions/Tasks.}
	\begin{enumerate}
		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem14-data-service-contract-c1}{1}
		{Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}

		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem14-data-service-contract-c3}{3}
		{Calculating profit and building the payoff table.}
	\end{enumerate}

	\subsubsection*{C1 - Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}
	\phantomsection
	\label{subsec:problem14-data-service-contract-c1}

	\DecisionElementsTable
	{Use standard data processing; Use automated data processing}
	{Standard-volume contract; High-volume contract}
	{Contract requirements confirmed after the processing-method decision}
	{Profit in thousand USD for each processing-method--contract-state pair}

	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{Revenue} contains \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based fixed components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{R}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	\GTenToneHL{cost}{Cost} contains \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based variable and fixed components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{C}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i}).
	\]

	Therefore,
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{payoff}{P}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right]
	-
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
	\right].
	\]

	\secInicio[sec:problem14-data-service-contract-c1-c3][sec:two-component-models]

	\subsection{Regional Parcel Delivery (C1--C3)}
	\label{sec:problem15-regional-parcel-delivery-c1-c3}

	A regional logistics company must select its standard or express delivery plan before conditions become high demand with light traffic or moderate demand with heavy traffic. Forecasts give the standard plan \(8\) hundreds of delivery batches in the first state and \(5\) in the second, and the express plan \(10\) and \(6\) hundreds, respectively.

	The standard service earns \(9\) thousand USD per hundred batches and express service earns \(11\); demand conditions add \(3\) per hundred batches under high demand or \(2\) under moderate demand. Service agreements also pay a fixed \(16\) thousand USD for the standard plan and \(24\) for express. Operating \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs} are \(4\) thousand USD per hundred batches for standard and \(5\) for express, while traffic adds \(1\) per hundred batches when light and \(3\) when heavy. Distribution arrangements finally \GTenToneHL{cost}{cost} a fixed \(10\) thousand USD in the high-demand, light-traffic state and \(15\) in the moderate-demand, heavy-traffic state.

	\textbf{Questions/Tasks.}
	\begin{enumerate}
		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem15-regional-parcel-delivery-c1}{1}
		{Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}

		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem15-regional-parcel-delivery-c3}{3}
		{Calculating profit and building the payoff table.}
	\end{enumerate}

	\subsubsection*{C1 - Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}
	\phantomsection
	\label{subsec:problem15-regional-parcel-delivery-c1}

	\DecisionElementsTable
	{Use the standard delivery plan; Use the express delivery plan}
	{High demand with light traffic; Moderate demand with heavy traffic}
	{Demand and traffic conditions revealed after the delivery-plan decision}
	{Profit in thousand USD for each delivery-plan--state pair}

	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{Revenue} contains \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based variable components and
	an \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based fixed component:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{R}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right]
	+
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i}).
	\]

	\GTenToneHL{cost}{Cost} contains \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based variable components and a
	state-based fixed component:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{C}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right]
	+
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	Therefore,
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\GTenToneHL{payoff}{P}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		={}&
		\left\{
		\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\left[
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right]
		+
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
		\right\}
		\\
		&-
		\left\{
		\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\left[
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right]
		+
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right\}.
	\end{aligned}
	\]

	\secInicio[sec:problem15-regional-parcel-delivery-c1-c3][sec:three-component-models]

	\begin{CalculationLandscape}{14in}{11in}
\subsubsection*{C3 - Calculating profit and building the payoff table.}
	\phantomsection
	\label{subsec:problem15-regional-parcel-delivery-c3}

\textbf{1. Symbols, values, and meanings.}

\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.19\linewidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.25\linewidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.50\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule Symbol & Value & Meaning in this problem. \\ \midrule
\endhead
\(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_1}\) & --- & Use the standard delivery plan. \\
\(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_2}\) & --- & Use the express delivery plan. \\
\(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_1}\) & --- & High demand with light traffic. \\
\(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_2}\) & --- & Moderate demand with heavy traffic. \\
\(\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_1},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_1})\) & 8 hundreds of delivery batches & Forecast \GTenToneHL{quantity}{quantity} for \(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_1}\) under \(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_1}\). \\
\(\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_1})\) & 9 thousand USD per hundred delivery batches & Delivery service rate for \(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_1}\) (rate per unit). \\
\(\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_1})\) & 3 thousand USD per hundred delivery batches & Demand supplement for \(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_1}\) (rate per unit). \\
\(\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_1})\) & 16 thousand USD & Service agreement payment for \(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_1}\) (fixed amount). \\
\(\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_1})\) & 4 thousand USD per hundred delivery batches & Operating rate for \(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_1}\) (rate per unit). \\
\(\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_1})\) & 1 thousand USD per hundred delivery batches & Traffic-related rate for \(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_1}\) (rate per unit). \\
\(\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_1})\) & 10 thousand USD & Distribution arrangement \GTenToneHL{cost}{cost} for \(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_1}\) (fixed amount). \\

	\subsection{Solar Installation Programme (C1--C3)}
	\label{sec:problem16-solar-installation-programme-c1-c3}

	A renewable-energy company must offer a standard-panel or high-efficiency-panel programme before demand and permits resolve into strong commercial demand or limited residential demand. It forecasts \(7\) installation batches for standard panels in the strong state and \(4\) in the limited state, compared with \(6\) and \(3\) batches for high-efficiency panels.

	Standard panels earn \(12\) thousand USD per batch and high-efficiency panels \(18\), while the market adds \(4\) per batch in the strong state or \(2\) in the limited state. An incentive provides fixed \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} of \(20\) thousand USD under strong commercial demand and \(11\) under limited residential demand. Installation inputs \GTenToneHL{cost}{cost} \(6\) thousand USD per standard-panel batch or \(8\) per high-efficiency batch. Programme setup \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs} a fixed \(18\) thousand USD for standard panels or \(25\) for high-efficiency panels, and permits add fixed \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs} of \(9\) in the strong state or \(14\) in the limited state.

	\textbf{Questions/Tasks.}
	\begin{enumerate}
		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem16-solar-installation-programme-c1}{1}
		{Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}

		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem16-solar-installation-programme-c3}{3}
		{Calculating profit and building the payoff table.}
	\end{enumerate}

	\subsubsection*{C1 - Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}
	\phantomsection
	\label{subsec:problem16-solar-installation-programme-c1}

	\DecisionElementsTable
	{Offer the standard-panel programme; Offer the high-efficiency-panel programme}
	{Strong commercial demand; Limited residential demand}
	{Customer demand and permit conditions revealed after the programme decision}
	{Profit in thousand USD for each programme--market-state pair}

	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{Revenue} contains \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based variable components and
	a state-based fixed component:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{R}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right]
	+
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	\GTenToneHL{cost}{Cost} contains an \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based variable component and \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based
	and state-based fixed components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{C}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
	+
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
	+
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	Therefore,
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\GTenToneHL{payoff}{P}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		={}&
		\left\{
		\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\left[
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right]
		+
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right\}
		\\
		&-
		\left\{
		\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
		+
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
		+
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right\}.
	\end{aligned}
	\]

	\secInicio[sec:problem16-solar-installation-programme-c1-c3][sec:three-component-models]

	\subsection{Hybrid Conference Production (C1--C3)}
	\label{sec:problem17-hybrid-conference-production-c1-c3}

	An events company must choose a standard or immersive hybrid-conference package before attendance and sponsorship are known to be strong or limited. Production forecasts allocate \(6\) event segments to the standard package in the strong state and \(4\) in the limited state, while the immersive package uses \(8\) and \(5\) segments, respectively.

	Each standard segment earns \(13\) thousand USD and each immersive segment \(16\). Booking contracts add fixed \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} of \(14\) thousand USD for the standard package or \(22\) for the immersive package, while sponsors add a further fixed \(18\) in the strong state or \(9\) in the limited state. Delivery \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs} \(5\) thousand USD per segment under strong conditions and \(7\) under limited conditions. Package setup adds a fixed \GTenToneHL{cost}{cost} of \(24\) for standard or \(37\) for immersive, and licensing adds a state-based fixed \GTenToneHL{cost}{cost} of \(8\) or \(13\), respectively.

	\textbf{Questions/Tasks.}
	\begin{enumerate}
		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem17-hybrid-conference-production-c1}{1}
		{Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}

		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem17-hybrid-conference-production-c3}{3}
		{Calculating profit and building the payoff table.}
	\end{enumerate}

	\subsubsection*{C1 - Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}
	\phantomsection
	\label{subsec:problem17-hybrid-conference-production-c1}

	\DecisionElementsTable
	{Use the standard hybrid package; Use the immersive hybrid package}
	{Strong attendance and sponsorship; Limited attendance and sponsorship}
	{Attendance and sponsorship conditions revealed after the package decision}
	{Profit in thousand USD for each production-package--state pair}

	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{Revenue} contains an \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based variable component and
	\GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based fixed components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{R}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
	+
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
	+
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	\GTenToneHL{cost}{Cost} contains a state-based variable component and \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and
	state-based fixed components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{C}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	+
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
	+
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	Therefore,
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\GTenToneHL{payoff}{P}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		={}&
		\left\{
		\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
		+
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
		+
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right\}
		\\
		&-
		\left\{
		\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		+
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
		+
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right\}.
	\end{aligned}
	\]

\end{tabular}}

	\subsection{Cold-Storage Export Contract (C1--C3)}
	\label{sec:problem18-cold-storage-export-contract-c1-c3}

	A food-export company must choose a modular or large cold-storage facility before export demand is strong or limited. Capacity and demand forecasts give the modular facility \(9\) storage batches under strong demand and \(5\) under limited demand, and the large facility \(12\) and \(7\) batches, respectively.

	Export conditions set \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} at \(10\) thousand USD per batch under strong demand or \(7\) under limited demand. The chosen capacity earns fixed \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} of \(20\) thousand USD for the modular facility or \(30\) for the large facility, and the export programme adds fixed \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} of \(16\) in the strong state or \(8\) in the limited state. Operating rates are \(4\) thousand USD per modular batch and \(3\) per large-facility batch; energy adds \(2\) per batch under strong demand or \(4\) under limited demand. Preparing the configuration also \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs} a fixed \(28\) thousand USD for modular storage or \(42\) for large storage.

	\textbf{Questions/Tasks.}
	\begin{enumerate}
		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem18-cold-storage-export-contract-c1}{1}
		{Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}

		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem18-cold-storage-export-contract-c3}{3}
		{Calculating profit and building the payoff table.}
	\end{enumerate}

	\subsubsection*{C1 - Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}
	\phantomsection
	\label{subsec:problem18-cold-storage-export-contract-c1}

	\DecisionElementsTable
	{Use the modular cold-storage facility; Use the large cold-storage facility}
	{Strong export demand; Limited export demand}
	{Export demand and energy-market conditions revealed after the facility decision}
	{Profit in thousand USD for each storage-facility--state pair}

	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{Revenue} contains a state-based variable component and \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and
	state-based fixed components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{R}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	+
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
	+
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	\GTenToneHL{cost}{Cost} contains \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based variable components and an
	\GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based fixed component:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{C}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right]
	+
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i}).
	\]

	Therefore,
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\GTenToneHL{payoff}{P}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		={}&
		\left\{
		\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		+
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
		+
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right\}
		\\
		&-
		\left\{
		\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\left[
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right]
		+
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
		\right\}.
	\end{aligned}
	\]

	\secInicio[sec:problem18-cold-storage-export-contract-c1-c3][sec:three-component-models]

	\begin{CalculationLandscape}{14in}{11in}

	\section{Complete Four-Component Models}
	\label{sec:complete-four-component-models}

	\SectionProblemsTableOfContents{
		\SectionProblemLink{sec:problem19-smart-manufacturing-contract-c1-c3}{Smart Manufacturing Contract (C1--C3)} \\
		\SectionProblemLink{sec:problem20-distribution-centre-automation-c1-c3}{Distribution Centre Automation (C1--C3)} \\

	}

	\subsection{Smart Manufacturing Contract (C1--C3)}
	\label{sec:problem19-smart-manufacturing-contract-c1-c3}

	A manufacturer must select conventional or automated assembly before conditions become strong demand with favourable inputs or limited demand with expensive inputs. Forecast production is \(8\) batches for conventional assembly in the favourable state and \(5\) in the expensive-input state, versus \(11\) and \(6\) batches for automation.

	Conventional batches earn a system-based \(12\) thousand USD each and automated batches \(15\); market conditions add \(4\) per batch in the favourable state or \(2\) in the expensive-input state. The contract also pays fixed \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} of \(18\) thousand USD for conventional assembly or \(28\) for automation, while a market incentive adds fixed \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} of \(14\) or \(7\) in the respective states.

	Operating rates are \(5\) thousand USD per conventional batch and \(6\) per automated batch. Materials add \(2\) per batch under favourable inputs or \(4\) under expensive inputs. Setup \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs} are fixed at \(24\) thousand USD for conventional assembly and \(38\) for automation, while compliance adds a fixed \(9\) in the favourable state or \(15\) in the expensive-input state.

	\textbf{Questions/Tasks.}
	\begin{enumerate}
		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem19-smart-manufacturing-contract-c1}{1}
		{Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}

		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem19-smart-manufacturing-contract-c3}{3}
		{Calculating profit and building the payoff table.}
	\end{enumerate}

	\subsubsection*{C1 - Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}
	\phantomsection
	\label{subsec:problem19-smart-manufacturing-contract-c1}

	\DecisionElementsTable
	{Use the conventional assembly system; Use the automated assembly system}
	{Strong demand with favourable input conditions; Limited demand with expensive inputs}
	{Demand and input-market conditions revealed after the production-system decision}
	{Profit in thousand USD for each production-system--state pair}

	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{Revenue} contains \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based variable components and
	\GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based fixed components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{R}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right]
	+
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
	+
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	\GTenToneHL{cost}{Cost} also contains \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based variable components and
	\GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based fixed components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{C}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right]
	+
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
	+
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	Therefore,
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\GTenToneHL{payoff}{P}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		={}&
		\left\{
		\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\left[
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right]
		+
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
		+
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right\}
		\\
		&-
		\left\{
		\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\left[
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right]
		+
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
		+
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right\}.
	\end{aligned}
	\]

	\subsection{Distribution Centre Automation (C1--C3)}
	\label{sec:problem20-distribution-centre-automation-c1-c3}

	A retailer must choose manual, semi-automated, or robotic distribution before order volume and energy prices resolve into high volume with favourable energy or moderate volume with expensive energy. Forecast quantities, in hundreds of order batches, are \(8\) and \(5\) for manual distribution, \(11\) and \(7\) for semi-automation, and \(14\) and \(8\) for robotics across those respective states.

	The configurations earn \(12\), \(15\), and \(18\) thousand USD per hundred order batches, respectively, because their service capabilities differ. The market adds \(4\) per hundred batches under favourable energy or \(2\) under expensive energy. Service contracts provide fixed \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} of \(16\), \(24\), and \(35\) thousand USD for manual, semi-automated, and robotic distribution, respectively; delivery incentives add fixed \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} of \(15\) in the high-volume state or \(8\) in the moderate-volume state.

	Operating rates are \(5\), \(6\), and \(8\) thousand USD per hundred batches for the three respective configurations. Energy adds \(2\) per hundred batches in the favourable state or \(4\) in the expensive state. Installation creates fixed \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs} of \(22\), \(34\), and \(50\) thousand USD for manual, semi-automated, and robotic distribution, respectively, while compliance adds fixed \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs} of \(9\) under high volume or \(16\) under moderate volume.

	\textbf{Questions/Tasks.}
	\begin{enumerate}
		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem20-distribution-centre-automation-c1}{1}
		{Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}

		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem20-distribution-centre-automation-c3}{3}
		{Calculating profit and building the payoff table.}
	\end{enumerate}

	\subsubsection*{C1 - Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}
	\phantomsection
	\label{subsec:problem20-distribution-centre-automation-c1}

	\DecisionElementsTable
	{Use the manual distribution configuration; Use the semi-automated distribution configuration; Use the robotic distribution configuration}
	{High order volume with favourable energy prices; Moderate order volume with expensive energy}
	{Order volume and energy-market conditions revealed after the distribution-configuration decision}
	{Profit in thousand USD for each distribution-configuration--state pair}

	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{Revenue} contains \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based variable components and
	\GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based fixed components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{R}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right]
	+
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
	+
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	\GTenToneHL{cost}{Cost} also contains \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based variable components and
	\GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based fixed components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{C}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right]
	+
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
	+
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	Therefore,
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\GTenToneHL{payoff}{P}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		={}&
		\left\{
		\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\left[
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right]
		+
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
		+
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right\}
		\\
		&-
		\left\{
		\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\left[
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right]
		+
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
		+
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right\}.
	\end{aligned}
	\]

\(\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_1},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_1})\) & 8 hundreds of order batches & Forecast \GTenToneHL{quantity}{quantity} for \(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_1}\) under \(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_1}\). \\
\(\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_1})\) & 12 thousand USD per hundred order batches & Distribution service rate for \(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_1}\) (rate per unit). \\

\end{CalculationLandscape}


	\subsection{Electric Vehicle Charging Network (C1--C3)}
	\label{sec:problem21-electric-vehicle-charging-network-c1-c3}

	An energy company must build a neighbourhood or rapid-charging network before utilisation and electricity prices become high and favourable, moderate and standard, or low and expensive. Forecast quantities, in hundreds of charging-session batches, are \(10\), \(7\), and \(4\) for the neighbourhood network across those states, and \(14\), \(9\), and \(5\) for the rapid network.

	Neighbourhood service earns a network-based \(11\) thousand USD per hundred charging-session batches and rapid charging earns \(16\). Utilisation conditions add \(4\), \(3\), and \(2\) thousand USD per hundred batches in the high, moderate, and low states, respectively. Network service agreements provide fixed \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} of \(18\) thousand USD for the neighbourhood network or \(30\) for rapid charging, while utilisation incentives add fixed \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} of \(16\), \(10\), and \(5\) in the respective states.

	Equipment \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs} \(4\) thousand USD per hundred batches for the neighbourhood network or \(7\) for rapid charging. Electricity adds \(2\), \(3\), and \(5\) thousand USD per hundred batches in the respective states. Infrastructure creates a fixed \GTenToneHL{cost}{cost} of \(25\) thousand USD for the neighbourhood design or \(45\) for rapid charging, while state-dependent maintenance adds fixed \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs} of \(8\), \(12\), and \(18\), respectively. The company must preserve all eight components when computing each network--state \GTenToneHL{payoff}{profit}.

	\textbf{Questions/Tasks.}
	\begin{enumerate}
		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem21-electric-vehicle-charging-network-c1}{1}
		{Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}

		\CriterionTask
		{subsec:problem21-electric-vehicle-charging-network-c3}{3}
		{Calculating profit and building the payoff table.}
	\end{enumerate}

	\subsubsection*{C1 - Interpreting decision alternatives, events, consequences, and states.}
	\phantomsection
	\label{subsec:problem21-electric-vehicle-charging-network-c1}

	\DecisionElementsTable
	{Build the neighbourhood charging network; Build the rapid-charging network}
	{High utilisation with favourable electricity prices; Moderate utilisation with standard electricity prices; Low utilisation with expensive electricity}
	{Charging demand and electricity-market conditions revealed after the network-design decision}
	{Profit in thousand USD for each charging-network--state pair}

	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{Revenue} contains \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based variable components and
	\GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based fixed components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{R}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right]
	+
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
	+
	\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	\GTenToneHL{cost}{Cost} also contains \GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based variable components and
	\GTenToneHL{alternative}{alternative}-based and state-based fixed components:
	\[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{C}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	=
	\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\left[
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
	\right]
	+
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
	+
	\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j}).
	\]

	Therefore,
	\[
	\begin{aligned}
		\GTenToneHL{payoff}{P}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		={}&
		\left\{
		\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\left[
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{revenue}{r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right]
		+
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
		+
		\GTenToneHL{revenue}{f_r}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right\}
		\\
		&-
		\left\{
		\GTenToneHL{quantity}{q}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i},\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\left[
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})+\GTenToneHL{cost}{c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right]
		+
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{alternative}{A_i})
		+
		\GTenToneHL{cost}{f_c}(\GTenToneHL{state}{S_j})
		\right\}.
	\end{aligned}
	\]

	\secInicio[sec:problem21-electric-vehicle-charging-network-c1-c3][sec:complete-four-component-models]

	The negative \GTenToneHL{payoff}{payoff} for the neighbourhood charging network under low
	utilisation represents a loss because the \GTenToneHL{revenue}{revenue} earned does not cover the
	variable and fixed \GTenToneHL{cost}{costs}.
